Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

Giá dầu duy trì ở mức cao, tiếp tục hỗ 
trợ biên lợi nhuận khí khô của GAS

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi mở rộng dư địa 
phát triển mỏ cận biên, tạo động lực 
tăng nguồn khí cho GAS trong dài 
hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
96,300 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng trưởng tích cực 44% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ (1) giá bán khí tăng 
và (2) tăng trưởng sản lượng tích cực từ mảng LNG. Trong khi đó, LNST-CĐTS tăng 
chậm hơn, ở mức 23% YoY, do ghi nhận khoản hoàn nhập dự phòng nợ phải thu khó 
đòi thấp hơn so với cùng kỳ năm trước.

Giá dầu Brent tăng trở lại lên vùng 95 USD/thùng trong bối cảnh xung đột Mỹ - Iran 
tiếp tục leo thang, làm gián đoạn nguồn cung dầu tại Trung Đông, trong khi lưu 
lượng tàu qua Eo biển Hormuz vẫn ở mức thấp và tồn kho dầu toàn cầu cùng Kho 
Dự trữ Chiến lược Mỹ liên tục suy giảm. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá dầu duy trì vùng 85 - 
95 USD/thùng trong ngắn hạn, qua đó tiếp tục hỗ trợ biên lợi nhuận mảng khí 
thượng nguồn của GAS nhờ chênh lệch giữa giá bán khí theo giá dầu và giá miệng 
giếng cố định tại các mỏ lâu đời.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi tăng ưu đãi cho các mỏ cận biên, giúp cải thiện hiệu quả đầu 
tư và thúc đẩy phát triển nguồn khí mới. GAS có thể hưởng lợi từ sản lượng khí bổ 
sung qua hệ thống hạ tầng hiện hữu, nhưng tác động chủ yếu trong trung và dài 
hạn.

ra khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 96,300 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức 
upside 16.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Kết quả kinh doanh tích cực trong 
2Q2026

Giá urea hạ nhiệt, xuất khẩu tiếp tục 
là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính 
của DCM trong 2H2026

Giá khí đầu vào DCM 2028–2030F 
tăng do bổ sung nguồn khí giá cao từ 
Nam Du – U Minh

Luật Dầu khí mới cải thiện triển vọng 
nguồn khí đầu vào cho DCM trong 
dài hạn

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
42,100 VND/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Q2/2026 tăng 10% YoY, chủ yếu nhờ giá bán urea tăng trong bối cảnh thị 
trường chịu tác động từ các yếu tố địa chính trị. LNST-CĐTS tăng 36% YoY, được hỗ 
trợ bởi giá bán đầu ra tăng nhanh hơn chi phí khí đầu vào.

Giá urea thế giới sau khi tăng cao trong Q2/2026 đã điều chỉnh về vùng trước chiến 
tranh do nhu cầu suy yếu và nguồn cung Trung Quốc quay trở lại. Đối với DCM, xuất 
khẩu urea tiếp tục là động lực dẫn dắt doanh thu chính, trong khi giá và sản lượng 
urea nội địa được kỳ vọng đi ngang trong 2H2026 do đây không phải mùa cao điểm 
tiêu thụ trong nước.

Chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng 10% dự báo giá khí đầu vào DCM giai đoạn 2028–2030F, 
phản ánh việc bổ sung nguồn khí Nam Du – U Minh từ Q4/2028. Giá khí Nam Du – U 
Minh dự kiến 11.56 USD/mmBTU, cao hơn ~35% so với các nguồn hiện hữu, tạo áp 
lực tăng chi phí đầu vào.

Luật Dầu khí sửa đổi sẽ rút ngắn quy trình phê duyệt FDP và bổ sung ưu đãi cho các 
mỏ khó khai thác, qua đó hỗ trợ đầu tư E&P và duy trì sản lượng dầu khí trong 
nước. Đối với DCM, tác động dự kiến mang tính gián tiếp và có độ trễ, chủ yếu thông 
qua cải thiện nguồn cung khí nội địa, chưa phải động lực thúc đẩy lợi nhuận đáng 
kể trong ngắn hạn.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu DCM chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị MUA với mức giá mục tiêu 42,100 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
29.5% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026.

Doanh thu và LNST-CĐTS 2Q2026 
tiếp tục tăng trưởng tốt, được hỗ trợ 
bởi đà tăng của crack spread

Biên lợi nhuận duy trì ở mức cao 
trong ngắn hạn nhờ crack spread 
bùng nổ, nhưng có thể biến động

Biên lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn chịu 
áp lực từ rủi ro dư cung dầu

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
32,200 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Q2/2026, doanh thu BSR tăng 60% YoY nhờ giá nhiên liệu tăng mạnh trong 
bối cảnh nguồn cung gián đoạn do xung đột Trung Đông. LNST-CĐTS tăng 807% 
YoY, được hỗ trợ bởi crack spread của bốn sản phẩm lọc dầu chủ lực tăng mạnh do 
nguồn cung thắt chặt.

Xung đột quân sự Mỹ – Iran gây gián đoạn nghiêm trọng dòng chảy dầu qua Eo biển 
Hormuz, thắt chặt nguồn cung dầu thô và thành phẩm, đẩy giá Brent lên quanh 100 
USD/thùng và đưa crack spread tại châu Á đạt đỉnh và duy trì ở vùng cao từ tháng 3 
đến nay. Diễn biến crack spread thời gian tới sẽ phụ thuộc đáng kể vào diễn biến 
chiến sự tại Eo biển Hormuz và tốc độ khôi phục lưu thông qua Eo biển này.

Triển vọng lợi nhuận trung và dài hạn của BSR chịu áp lực khi cán cân cung – cầu 
dầu toàn cầu được dự báo chuyển sang dư cung trong trung và dài hạn. Tuy nhiên, 
nhu cầu tái tích lũy tồn kho dầu toàn cầu có thể phần nào hấp thụ lượng cung dư 
thừa và hạn chế mức độ thu hẹp crack spread.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp FCFF và EV/EBITDA định giá cổ phiếu BSR chúng tôi đưa ra 
khuyến nghị Mua với mức giá mục tiêu 32,200 VND/cổ phiếu, tương ứng mức upside 
16.9% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 11/09/2026. Chúng tôi áp dụng mức EV/EBITDA mục 
tiêu là 15.8 lần, bằng với mức chuẩn trung bình 5 năm của cổ phiếu.

BÁO CÁO NGẮN CƠ HỘI ĐẦU TƯ

15/09/2026

CTCP Nhựa Tiền Phong (NTP) 
Tăng tốc trong chu kỳ thuận lợi

Mua 
Giá mục tiêu VND 73.200 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 1 50.000 – 51.400 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 1 44% 
Vùng hỗ trợ/Điểm mua 2 42.000 – 44.000 
Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng 2 70%

Lưu ý: 
Nhà đầu tư cần kết hợp nhận định xu hướng thị 
trường chung, mức độ hấp dẫn của các điểm 
nhấn đầu tư và khẩu vị rủi ro của mình để quản 
lý tỷ trọng tại từng điểm mua cho phù hợp. 
Do việc xác định mỗi điểm mua được dựa trên 
các khung thời gian khác nhau nên chúng tôi 
không đưa ra mức dừng lỗ cố định. Nhìn chung, 
mức dừng lỗ nên được đặt chặt hơn cho điểm 
mua gần (5-7%) và rộng hơn cho điểm mua xa 
(10-15%).

ĐIỂM NHẤN ĐẦU TƯ 
Kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ tăng trưởng mạnh hơn so với giai đoạn trước. KBSV 
dự báo sản lượng tiêu thụ của NTP tăng 2,3% CAGR giai đoạn 2026 - 2028 nhờ (1) 
Đẩy mạnh giải ngân đầu tư hạ tầng tại miền Bắc (2) gia tăng đầu tư hạ tầng tại các 
địa phương thu hút FDI (3) tăng tốc đơn hàng mảng dự án.

Giá hạt nhựa sẽ gây áp lực biên lợi nhuận nửa cuối 2026 nhưng không làm thay 
đổi triển vọng dài hạn. Mặc dù duy trì chính sách dự phòng nguyên liệu cao và đa 
dạng hóa nhà cung cấp, nhưng NTP vẫn đối mặt với rủi ro thu hẹp lợi nhuận, 
nhưng sẽ không làm giảm đáng kể triển vọng tăng trưởng trung hạn. KBSV cho 
rằng tình trạng dư cung hạt nhựa tại Trung Quốc vẫn sẽ duy trì trong trung hạn và 
xung đột được giải quyết, giảm áp lực biên lợi nhuận toàn ngành.

Catalyst thoái vốn kỳ vọng tái định giá doanh nghiệp. Theo kế hoạch thoái vốn 
Nhà nước, SCIC dự kiến thoái toàn bộ 37,1% cổ phần tại NTP . Chúng tôi đánh giá 
việc hiện thực hóa thương vụ có thể giúp NTP (1) thu hút nhà đầu tư chiến lược 
mới; (2) cộng hưởng mô hình kinh doanh; (3) tái định giá doanh nghiệp.

Hoạt động kinh doanh  Diễn biến doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất

NTP là một trong những doanh nghiệp đầu 
ngành ống nhựa tại Việt Nam, với thị phần 
khoảng 31% toàn quốc và 60% tại miền Bắc. 
Hoạt động kinh doanh cốt lõi tập trung vào 
sản xuất và phân phối ống nhựa cùng các sản 
phẩm phụ tùng. Nhờ lợi thế về thương hiệu, 
quy mô và mạng lưới phân phối, NTP duy trì 
vị thế dẫn đầu và nền tảng tăng trưởng ổn 
định qua các năm.

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KB Securities Vietnam

8,3 lần thấp hơn trung bình 5 năm của cổ

25,950 VNĐ/CP  
 Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng tăng trưởng tín d ụng 2026 đ ạt 27%, với động lực chính 
đến từ phân khúc KHDN, đ ặc biệt là t ừ nhóm FDI trong b ối cảnh dòng v ốn FDI vào 
Việt Nam và hoạt động xuất nhập khẩu duy trì tích cực. Xét về lĩnh vực, cho vay kinh 
doanh BĐS kỳ vọng sẽ tăng trưởng vượt trội khi ngân hàng tham gia vào thu xếp vốn 
cho các dự án hạ tầng và d ự án BĐS ưu tiên , trong khi các m ảng kinh doanh khác 
tăng trưởng ở mức khả quan.

Chúng tôi duy trì dự phóng NIM giảm so với năm 2025 do áp l ực huy động khiến chi 
phí vốn duy trì ở mặt bằng cao, trong khi lãi su ất cho vay dự kiến ở mức cạnh tranh 
để ưu tiên mục tiêu tăng trưởng tín dụng.

Chúng tôi dự phóng tỷ lệ nợ xấu của ngân hàng sẽ tăng 11bps YoY trong bối cảnh lãi 
suất đang neo ở mức cao và kỳ vọng ngân hàng sẽ tiếp tục duy trì chính sách trích 
lập dự phòng thận trọng nhằm củng cố bộ đệm, qua đó hoàn thành mục tiêu duy 
trì tỷ lệ bao phủ nợ xấu ở mức trên 100%.

chúng tôi tiếp tục duy trì khuyến nghị MUA với cổ phiếu MBB với giá mục tiêu 25,950

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX cho thấy xu hướng hồi phục trong tuần qua nhưng với diễn biến khá giằng co, 
được thể hiện qua sự hình thành của một mẫu nến "spinning" trên đồ thị tuần. Trên khung 
ngày, bức tranh có phần tích cực hơn khi chỉ số đã cho phản ứng với vùng hỗ trợ quanh 
1790 và hồi phục trở lại, phản ánh sự nhập cuộc khá chủ động của lực cầu. Vùng kháng cự 
gần quanh 1850 bắt đầu khiến áp lực bán có phần gia tăng trong phiên cuối tuần và diễn 
biến rung lắc có thể sẽ còn tiếp diễn. Mặc dù vậy, khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được 
bảo lưu, nhịp giảm điểm vừa qua nhiều khả năng chỉ là bước điều chỉnh tích lũy trước khi 
VNINDEX thử thách lại và có cơ hội vượt vùng đỉnh cũ.

## Document 281: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

offers investors compelling risk-reward trade off

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations 
Our policy rate outlook for the Fed, ECB and BoJ

tariffs, long-term inflation expectations remain subdued

### Page 3

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

offsetting AI-driven effects on bond yields

## Document 312: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Eurozone: Marked increase in inflation in September 2026,
to a 3-year high
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022. Unit: inflation change in percentage point, point for confidence surveys.
Eurozone: Inflation (incl. & excl. Energy)

## Document 327: France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

2027 Budget: Fiscal consolidation must now resume

The 2027 budget, adopted by the French Council of Ministers today, calls for a reduction in France’s fiscal 
deficit to 5% of GDP in 2027 (down from 5.4% in 2026). This is a first step toward s the multi-year fiscal 
consolidation needed to stabilise the public debt-to-GDP ratio. This ratio is expected to rise further in 2027 
and until the deficit is reduced to 3% of GDP. Furthermore, given the increase in some expenditures (+0.6 pp 
of GDP for debt service and defence spending), the projected fiscal effort amounts to 1 pp. Nearly one-third 
of this effort would come from higher compulsory levies, and two -thirds from curbing government and 
social spending.

2027 Draft finance law: A step in the right direction. The government is forecasting an improvement in the fiscal 
deficit from 5.4% of GDP in 2026 to 5% in 2027. This projection is based on a credible growth assumption of 1% 
(see our analysis of French growth drivers). To achieve this target, compulsory levies (CL) are expected to rise to 
44.2% of GDP, an increase of 0.3 pp (the same increase as in 2026, compared with +0.9 pp in 2025). The public 
spending-to-GDP ratio would decline by 0.2 pp to 56.9%. The effort required to reduce the deficit to 5% is greater 
than it appears. Indeed, some expenditures will increase (interest payments by 0.4 pp and defence spending by 
0.2 pp). Consequently, the fiscal effort for next year amounts not to 0.4 pp, but to 1 pp of GDP. One-third of this will 
be achieved through increased CL, and two-thirds through spending cuts. Social spending will contribute, notably 
through the cancellation of EUR 3 billion in social contribution reductions for low-wage earners, as well as efforts 
totaling EUR 6 billion each in the areas of pensions and healthcare. Social spending shows the largest deviation 
from pre-COVID levels, with an increase representing 1.2 pp of GDP, nearly half of the gap between the 2026 
budget balance and a 3% deficit. Government spending, excluding interest payments and defence, is expected to 
be frozen in nominal terms.

A compromise seems likely despite a tight schedule. The French National Assembly will start reviewing the 
budget on Tuesday 13 October. The government will need to ensure that a majority vote against the draft budget 
does not form. The current circumstances, including increasing sovereign bond yields and the upcoming 
presidential election, call for a consensus based on fiscal consolidation of the size proposed by the government. If 
Parliament does not pass a budget by the end of the year, the government will have to choose between 
implementing the government’s budget proposal by executive order ( only possible if there was no parliamentary 
vote) or through a special law (as in 2025 and 2026). A special law would enable tax collection , while the 
government and social security agencies could continue to issue debt. In this case, the 2026 budget would apply 
in 2027 (without indexation) until Parliament passes a budget in the first few weeks of the year. This must occur 
before the end of February (when Parliament’s session ends, earlier than usual due to the presidential election).

The one-year postponement of the 5% budget deficit target has a measurable impact. The gap between the 
budget adopted for 2026 (deficit of 5% of GDP) and the actual budget outcome (deficit of 5.4%) is due to 
unforeseeable circumstances (the conflict in Iran  and extreme drought). However, postponing the deficit target  
previously set for 2026 to 2027 comes at a cost: 1) the public debt-to-GDP ratio is expected to reach 121% in 2027 
according to our forecasts (1 pp above our scenario with a 5% target in 2026 and a deficit reduced to 4.5% in 2027), 
and 2) the (upcoming) postponement to 2032 of the 3% of GDP deficit target (a commitment that most presidential 
candidates plan to make) rather than in 2029 (the government had committed to this with the European Commission 
in 2024). In 2032, the public debt-to-GDP ratio would stabilise at 124% of GDP (instead of 121.5% if the deficit had 
been reduced to 5% of GDP as early as 2026 and to 3% of GDP by the end of the decade).

Another delay in fiscal consolidation would further erode France’s credibility. This new delay could stem 
either from a decision by the government formed in May 2027 to immediately implement part of its agenda, or from 
the enactment of a special law that would remain in effect until (just after) the likely legislative elections in June  
2027  or  for  the  entire  year.  If  the  government  emerging  from  the  elections  were  to  resume  fiscal  
consolidation only in 2028 (with a deficit of 5% of GDP that it projects to reduce to 3% by 2032), public debt would 
reach 126% of GDP by that date (a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in an additional 2 pp of 
debt). This further delay could potentially undermine the credibility of the next government, given that budget 
targets were  not met in three out of four years between 2023 and 2026, a situation that would be costly amid a 
sharp rise in long-term interest rates. Furthermore, a higher deficit would increase the amount of debt to be issued 
within an already  tight schedule. The Agence France Trésor (AFT) has announced debt issuances totaling EUR 
340 billion in 2027 (an  increase  of  EUR  20  billion  compared  to  2026).  This  increase  is  due  to  the  maturing  
COVID  related  debt (beginning in 2027 and continuing largely through 2030), while the amount of debt to be 
issued to finance the deficit will remain stable compared to 2026. A decrease from this last driver would be needed 
in subsequent years so that  the AFT’s debt issuance can stabilise.

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of the Advanced Economies Team, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

► 聯準會9月升息1碼至3.75%-4.00%，最新點
陣圖顯示2026年仍有再升息一碼空間 ，2027
年傾向維持利率不變 ，2028年後才逐步回到
降息趨勢 。主席華許重申抑制通膨目標 ，升
息立場官員數明顯較前次會議增加 ，凸顯聯
準會鷹派政策態度 ，惟利率路徑較市場原先
預期的連續緊縮和緩，市場偏利多解讀。
► 聯準會 本次亦上修 2026-2027年GDP成長預
估，下修失業率預測 ，反映美國 經濟與勞動
市場尚未明顯轉弱；另一方面 ，能源價格上
升與成本轉嫁使整體及核心通膨預估再度上

修；景氣具韌性 、通膨回落速度偏慢 ，使聯
準會仍有條件維持較緊縮的政策立場 。在高
利率環 境可 能延 續 下，股 票評價 仍面 臨壓
力，配置上可相對偏向獲利與現金流穩定 、
防禦性較高的產業；債券則以中短天期 、高
信評企業債為主要方向 ，把握目前較高的收
益率。長天期公債仍受通膨 、財政紀律及期
限溢酬上升影響 ，宜待通膨與景氣出現更明
確降溫訊號後，再提高配置。

► 日本8月CPI年增1.9%，油價上漲帶來的外溢
影響，逐漸反應在消費成本上。7月勞工平均
薪資較去年同期成長 4.7%，創1997年1月以
來最大增幅；薪資增速追上通膨 ，帶動實質
薪資增長 。此外 ，第二季 GDP季增年率達
1.4%優於預期，8月製造業PMI因AI及半導體
需求暢旺上升，7月出口年增率高達23.2%，
創2022年10月來最快增速 。整體通膨升溫加
上景氣復甦，為日銀升息提供底氣。
► 日本央行會後 聲明稿表示：金融環境實質利
率偏低 ，將視情況調整貨幣政策 ，並強調升
息後金融環境仍維持寬鬆 ，未如市場預期的
鷹派，使日股持續上漲 、日圓回貶 。考量日
銀呵護 寬鬆金融環境 ，侷限日圓續強力 道，
但在美 國施 壓與 日 銀未來 仍可能 升息 背景
下，日圓難回到過去極弱 狀態。相較過去 ，
現在升值的日圓對日本出口 、科技股壓力可
控，日本10年公債殖利率來到3%，利率上升
有利 金融 股 ，投 資 短期或 以金融 、內 需為

主，科技、半導體股逢回可分批布局。
經濟與通膨升溫，日銀加快緊縮步調

投資焦點低波價值防禦
► 美、歐、日主要央行連袂升息 ，加上美伊衝
突惡化 ，國際油價高漲 ，通膨疑慮與成熟國
家債務壓力 ，使全球多數國家公債殖利率衝
高，市場擔憂高利率與高油價可能拖累經濟
成長，全球股債匯市承壓 、波動上升 ，並以
此前領漲多頭行情的晶片 、半導體和科技股
最明顯 。觀察今年以來 ，不止股市波動度上
升，債市的波動更不亞於 股票市場 ，統計過
去，當VIX恐慌指數與MOVE債券波動度走高
時，低波動或防禦股的表現 ，往往比成長股
更好，顯示當市場震盪加劇時 ，資金傾向轉
往穩健資產停泊。
► 最近一次聯準會升息 ，為2022 年因疫情爆
發、供應鏈中斷 ，導致通膨大幅上揚 ，迫使
央行採取緊縮貨幣政策 ，和目前通膨上升促
央行升息背景 類似。前次聯準會啟動升息後
數月，成長股 、費城半導體股表現受抑 ，防
禦股、價值股和低波動族群 ，在剛開始升息
的環境下 ，表現相對抗跌 。考量目前聯準 會
籠罩在升息陰霾下，可增加相關類股配置。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 最近一次聯準會升息後，防禦低波高息和價值股突出美國股債波動度上升時，低波動、防禦股表現較佳

金融市場波動上升，增配低波、防禦價值股更穩健

### Page 8

8
► 觀察最近一季 ，資訊科技 、通訊服務與非核
心消費等High Beta類股，表現明顯落後於大
盤；反觀價值股 、防禦股和低波動等因子 ，
甚至核 心消 費與 高 股利等 傳統類 防禦 性產
業，報酬相對較 穩健，受資金青睞 。顯示在
經歷過 上半 年科 技 、半導 體股的 大漲 行情
後，市場預期高 利率與地緣政治 風險可能維
持更久時間 ，重新聚焦在獲利持久、現金易
落袋為安的防禦型資產，更能持盈保泰。
► 從預估本 益比看，目前金融僅 16倍、防禦股
17.5倍、價值股 (以IWD ETF代表)17.9倍、
低波動 (以USMV ETF代表)19.6倍，皆低於
標普500指數 現在 的21.3倍，近月資金流向
物美價廉的低波動價值 、防禦族群，評價越
低、漲幅越高 。展望後市，AI長線基本面仍
持續看好 ，股市回檔或為評價 修正而 非盈餘
下滑，市場波動度提升之際 ，建議不過度 集
中於單一 產業或 個股，多元配置適度增加 低
波動 、 防禦 和價 值股，分 批布局 以控管 風

險，料將更能在震盪加劇時安穩求利。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，利率創高添股市波動風險 ，加上成熟國家債務攀升 ，科技巨擘亦舉債
投入資本支出 ，未來獲利與變現 能力持續被市場檢視 ，金融市場震盪不減 。美伊衝突升溫，近期可
透過防禦、低波動或價值 股來降低風險；科技 及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利基本面佳 ，長線發
展可期，宜採逢回分批布局方式，分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，歐洲國防軍工
仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融等產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國 10年期公債利率將於 4.5%-5.0%
震盪，可逢利率走升時，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款
能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。

債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金融、
科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息後，年底前或明年初仍可能再度緊縮，日圓震盪加大偏升。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪加大偏升
商品
 沙烏地阿拉伯關閉輸油管線 ，加上荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張 ，能源供需失衡 ，油價年底前維持 偏高水位。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力 與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

## Document 424: USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

Personal consumption (third estimate) +3.8% (qoq ar) for Q2 vs. GS +3.4%, median
forecast +3.4%, prior revised +0.7%
Personal income +0.2% for August vs. GS +0.5%, median forecast +0.5%, prior revised

MAIN POINTS:
1. The core PCE index increased +0.25% in August, somewhat below expectations, and
the year-over-year rate increased 3.01%. We estimate that the Dallas Fed’s trimmed
mean PCE measure increased 0.16% month over month and 2.20% year over year.
2. Year-over-year core PCE was revised downward 0.36pp in July to 2.98%, primarily
reflecting the methodological revisions to the calculation of the portfolio management
(-0.23pp), computer accessories and software (-0.11pp), and legal services (+0.01pp)

components of PCE. These downward revisions were larger than we expected, mostly
reflecting a larger revision to the portfolio management component than we expected.
3. Today’s inflation report, especially the downward revisions resulting from the
methodological changes, imply that core PCE inflation is likely to substantially
undershoot the median FOMC participant’s forecast of 3.4% for 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis.
We now expect a 3.0% increase for core PCE in 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis. Coupled with

comments from New York Fed President John Williams yesterday that “with the policy
action we took at our September meeting, there is no need for urgency, and we have
time to gather more information,” we now think that an October hike is unlikely.  We are
pushing back the second hike in our forecast to December, and we see a strong chance
that the FOMC will ultimately conclude that additional rate hikes are unnecessary.
4. Real GDP was revised up by 0.7pp to +2.2% in Q2 (quarter-over-quarter annualized),

largely reflecting upward revisions to consumption (+0.4pp to +3.8%) and investment
(+1.9pp to +4.6%) growth. The strength in consumer spending in Q2 likely continued to
reflect the boost to consumer cashflow from larger-than-usual tax refunds, but we
continue to expect consumer spending growth to slow somewhat in 2026Q4 as the fiscal
support fades and the headwind from higher energy prices remains. Stronger
investment growth reflected an upward revision to private fixed investment (+0.5pp to

+9.0%), driven by data center investment in the nonresidential structures category
(+1.9pp to +0.1%), and an upward revision to the contribution from inventories (+0.2pp
to -0.5pp).
5. Reflecting today’s annual revisions to the national accounts, real GDP was revised up
by 0.4pp to +2.5% in Q1 (quarter-over-quarter annualized), largely reflecting upward
revisions to the contributions from consumption and exports. Revisions for prior years
were modest: real GDP growth on a Q4/Q4 basis was unrevised for 2025 (+2.0%) and

2023 (+3.4%), and slightly revised down for 2024 (-0.2pp to +2.4%) and up for 2022
30 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs

### Page 3

(+0.1pp to +1.3%). 
6. Personal income increased 0.2% in August, below expectations. Year-over-year 
disposable personal income growth reached 4.8% in August. Personal spending rose 
0.9% in August, in line with consensus expectations. The savings rate decreased 0.5pp to 
4.1% in August, after a large 1.6pp upward revision to the savings rate last month driven 
by an upward revision to the level of disposable personal income (+1.2pp contribution) 
and a downward revision to personal spending as a share of DPI (+0.4pp). 
7. According to the Advance Economic Indicators Report, the goods trade deficit 
widened by $13.7bn to $132.6bn (seasonally adjusted) in August, well above 
expectations. Goods exports increased by $3.7bn to $203.4bn, largely driven by an 
increase in industrial supplies exports. Goods imports increased by $17.4bn to 
$336.1bn, driven by a large increase in industrial supplies and capital goods imports. 
8. According to the ADP report, private sector employment rose by 90k in September 
(month-over-month, seasonally adjusted), slightly above consensus expectations. 
Employment in the services sector rose 59k, primarily reflecting a 55k increase in 
education and health services and a 22k increase in leisure and hospitality employment 
that were partly offset by a 16k decrease in financial activities employment. 
Employment in goods-producing industries increased by 31k, driven by a 17k increase in 
manufacturing and a 15k increase in construction employment. ADP’s new median 
annual base pay measure, which better tracks other measures of wage growth than its 
gross pay measure, increased 3.0% for job stayers, unchanged from the August pace, 
and 4.8% for job changers, 0.1pp above the August pace. We left our forecast for August 
nonfarm payroll growth unchanged at +80k (vs. +90k consensus) ahead of Friday’s 
release. 
9. The details of the personal income and spending report were firmer than our previous 
GDP tracking assumptions. While the trade-related details of the Advance Economic 
Indicators report were much weaker than our previous assumptions, suggesting notably 
stronger imports growth in Q3 than we previously assumed, the inventories details were 
significantly stronger. On net, we lowered our Q3 GDP tracking estimate by 0.1pp to 
+3.3% (quarter-over-quarter annualized).

## Document 450: 【國泰證期研究部】半導體測試產業-Insertion 4 為CPO量產化的「最後一公里」-20261013

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】半導體測試產業-Insertion 4 為CPO量產化的「最後一公里」-20261013.pdf

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
2026/10/13

1 
產業更新 – 半導體測試產業 
Insertion 4 為 CPO 量產化的「最後一公里」 
What’s New – CPO 測試流程逐步走向「系統級出貨驗證」 
CPO 測試流程逐步從「單體功能驗證」走向「系統級出貨驗證」 ，測試對象由 PIC／EIC 等單一
元件，逐步擴展至  Optical Engine ，最終進入完整  CPO 系統的功能、效能與可靠度驗證。
Insertion 1–3 的核心目的，是在 CPO 整合程度與元件價值逐步提高的過程中，盡可能提早攔
截不良品，為後續 Insertion 4 的系統級出貨驗證建立良率基礎。Insertion 4 之所以重要，在於
它是 CPO 從「單體功能驗證」走向「系統級出貨驗證」的最後一道關鍵測試關卡，與前段測試
目的大不相同。 
Trends/Outlook – In 4 競爭關鍵轉向完整光電測試系統整合 
Insertion 4 的測試重點，在於完成 ASIC 與 Optical Engine 整合後，驗證整體 CPO 的光電訊
號傳輸是否符合規格。Insertion 4 必須同時確認兩條關鍵路徑：1) ASIC 與 Optical Engine 之間
的高速電性互聯 。2) Optical Engine 與 FA／FAU 光纖介面之間的光訊號發射 （Tx） 與接收 （Rx）。
在測試架構上，In4 可依光學與電性測試的整合程度，分為「光電分開測」 與 「光電同測」 。光電
分開測較容易沿用既有的半導體與光學測試設備 ， 但較難在相同條件下直接驗證完整的端到端光
電訊號鏈路；光電同測則是在同一測試環境中，同時建立高速電性與光學介面， 可更完整掌握 
Tx/Rx、BER、訊號品質以及溫度變化對整體系統的影響。 
Investment Summary 
綜觀 Insertion 4 供應鏈，海內外廠商皆有參與。Advantest 與 Teradyne 皆已將 CPO 最終封
裝測試納入光電測試布局。Advantest （6857 JP） 主要以既有 V93000 ATE 平台為核心建立
整合式測試架構；Teradyne（TER US）的 Photon 100 明確定位為矽光與 CPO 的光電自動
化量產測試平台。台廠方面，致茂（2360 TT）憑藉既有光學對位與量測能力，先切入 In4o 領
域，佈局涵蓋自動光學連接、校正清潔與插入損耗量測，將受惠於CPO 封裝後光學測試需求持
續增加；鴻勁 （7769 TT） 則聚焦 Handler 與溫控系統 （ATC），In4 光電同測設備將從 FY26 進
入量產型驗證階段，FY27 將持續隨客戶產品開發進度推進，雖 FY26 CPO 相關營收貢獻仍有
限，但將成為中長期成長動能，研究部預期鴻勁於高階 ATC Handler具領先市場地位，將能受惠
於後段測試對精密操作、溫控及自動化整合需求提升。

Insertion4 為 CPO 量產化的「最後一公里」 
CPO 測試流程逐步從「單體功能驗證」走向「系統級出貨驗證」 
CPO 測試流程逐步從 「單體功能驗證」 走向 「系統級出貨驗證」 ，測試對象由 PIC／EIC 等單一元
件，逐步擴展至 Optical Engine，最終進入完整 CPO 系統的功能、效能與可靠度驗證。Insertion 
4 之所以重要，在於它是 CPO 從 「單體功能驗證」 走向 「系統級出貨驗證」 的最後一道關鍵測試
關卡，與前段測試目的大不相同。 
在進入 Insertion 4 系統級測試之前，CPO 前段主要透過 Insertion 1–3 逐步篩選不良元件並確認
光電整合品質。Insertion 1 主要針對尚未整合的 PIC 與 EIC 進行晶圓／裸晶層級測試，分別確
認光學與電性功能是否符合規格，以篩選 Known Good Die （KGD） ，避免不良晶粒進入後續高成
本封裝 。Insertion 2 則發生在 PIC 與 EIC 完成鍵合後 ， 測試重點由單一元件轉向光電整合功能 ，
透過電性探針與光學耦合，同步驗證電轉光（E/O）與光轉電（O/E）訊號是否正常，並檢查鍵合
後的高速訊號與光學性能 。Insertion 3 則進一步來到 Optical Engine 階段 ， 在晶片切割並完成 FAU
／光纖等光學介面組裝後 ， 針對完整光引擎進行 Tx/Rx、 光功率 、BER、 多通道與高速介面等測試 ，
篩選出 Known Good Optical Engine（KGOE） 。因此，Insertion 1–3 的核心目的，是在 CPO 整合程
度與元件價值逐步提高的過程中，盡可能提早攔截不良品，為後續  Insertion 4 的系統級出貨驗
證建立良率基礎。

Insertion 4 競爭關鍵逐漸由單一設備性能，轉向完整光電測試系統的整合與自動化能力 
Insertion 4 的測試重點，在於完成 ASIC 與 Optical Engine 整合後，驗證整體 CPO 的光電訊號傳輸
是否符合規格。相較於前段測試著重於個別元件或 Optical Engine，Insertion 4 必須同時確認兩條
關鍵路徑：1) ASIC 與 Optical Engine 之間的高速電性互聯。2) Optical Engine 與 FA／FAU 光纖介
面之間的光訊號發射（Tx）與接收（Rx）。在 Tx 路徑中，需確認 ASIC 輸出的高速電訊號能否經
由 EIC 與 PIC 正確轉換為光訊號並透過光纖送出 ；Rx 路徑則需驗證外部光訊號能否經由光偵測
器與 EIC 轉換回電訊號，再正確傳送至 ASIC。隨著 CPO 傳輸速率與通道數持續提升，測試過程

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
產業報告

2026/10/13

3 
還需進一步考量 BER、訊號完整性、通道間干擾、功耗及溫度變化等因素，使 I4 從單一元件測
試提升至完整光電鏈路的系統級驗證。 
在測試架構上，I4 可依光學與電性測試的整合程度，分為「光電分開測」與「光電同測」 。光電
分開測是將電性與光學測試安排於不同測試步驟或測試站 ， 較容易沿用既有的半導體與光學測試
設備，但較難在相同條件下直接驗證完整的端到端光電訊號鏈路；光電同測則是在同一測試環境
中，同時建立高速電性與光學介面，直接驗證從 ASIC、Optical Engine 到 Fiber 的訊號傳輸，可
更完整掌握 Tx/Rx、BER、訊號品質以及溫度變化對整體系統的影響。 
因此，若 In4 朝光電同測發展，其技術門檻將不再只是  Tester 本身的測試能力，而是如何將  
Tester、Handler、Test Interface、Optical Interface 與 Thermal Control 整合至同一套量產測試平台 。
在兼顧測試精度的同時，設備還必須維持穩定的光學耦合、高速電性接觸與溫度控制，並縮短測
試時間 、 提高平行測試能力， 以滿足量產所需的 throughput。這使 In4 的競爭關鍵逐漸由單一設
備性能，轉向完整光電測試系統的整合與自動化能力。

競爭重點延伸至光學連接自動化、Handler／Interface 整合 
綜觀 Insertion 4 供應鏈，海內外廠商皆有參與。Advantest 與 Teradyne 皆已將 CPO 最終封裝測
試納入光電測試布局。Advantest 主要以既有 V93000 ATE 平台為核心，透過與光學探針、FAU、
Handler 與 Test Interface 生態系夥伴合作，建立可涵蓋  wafer、die 到最終 NPO／CPO package 
test 的整合式測試架構；Teradyne 的 Photon 100 明確定位為矽光與 CPO 的光電自動化量產測
試平台，將光學與電性量測整合進既有 UltraFLEXplus ATE，並可支援 wafer、Optical Engine 以及 
co-packaged module 等不同 insertion。整體而言，兩家公司在 I4 的競爭方向，都是從傳統「電
性 Tester」走向「光電整合測試單元」 ，隨 CPO 進入量產，兩家的競爭重點預期將不只在 Tester 
規格，而會進一步延伸至光學連接自動化、 Handler／Interface 整合、平行測試能力與整體  
throughput。 
台廠方面，致茂（2360 TT）憑藉既有光學對位與量測能力，先切入 In4o 領域，佈局涵蓋自動光
學連接、校正清潔與插入損耗量測，佈局領域完善，將受惠於 CPO 封裝後光學測試需求持續增
加；鴻勁（7769 TT）則聚焦 Handler 與溫控系統（ATC），In4 光電同測設備將從 FY26 進入量產型
驗證階段，FY27 將持續隨客戶產品開發進度推進，雖 FY26 CPO 相關營收貢獻仍有限，但將成為
中長期成長動能，研究部預期鴻勁於高階 ATC Handler 具領先市場地位，將能受惠於後段測試對
精密操作、溫控及自動化整合需求提升。

## Document 486: なぜ企業は同じ情報を何度も提出しなければならないのか

Source PDF: all report pdf/なぜ企業は同じ情報を何度も提出しなければならないのか.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026 年 10 月 16 日 全9 頁

なぜ企業は同じ情報を何度も提出 しなければ
ならないのか 
公的統計のデジタル化を問う①－負担軽減の「効果」

政策調査部 主任研究員 溝端 幹雄

[要約] 
 公的統計は、経済社会の実態を把握する上で重要な公共財である。しかし近年、回答
負担の重さなどを背景に、統計調査全体の回収率の低下が世界的な問題となっており、
日本も例外ではない。世帯系調査だけでなく企業系調査でも回収率が 低下し、標本の
偏りや推計精度の悪化を通じて、統計の信頼性と制度の持続可能性を脅かしている。 
 企業、とりわけ一定規模以上の企業は、複数の基幹統計調査・一般統計調査の対象と
なり得る。売上高・従業者数・設備投資などの類似項目でも、定義、対象範囲、対象
期間、締め切りが異なり、調査ごとに確認・加工する必要がある。負担は専任の統計
担当を置きにくい中小企業ほど固定費的に重く、統計だけでなく企業の「レポーティ
ング全体」を見据えれば、削減余地は大きい。 
 負担軽減の第一歩は測定 である。日本でも「官民の統計コスト削減」に取り組んだが 、
その後、同じ枠組みによる政府横断的な継続計測は確認できない。海外では、 標準コ
ストモデルや法制度に基づき、行政手続・政府の情報収集に伴う負担の計測が行われ
ている。日本の次期第 V 期公的統計基本計画では計測を制度に組み込み、 「測って減ら
す」PDCA を回すべきである。 
 デジタル化による負担軽減の効果は、①調査の電子化（オンライン回答） 、②調査自体
の削減（行政記録・企業データの連携） 、③調査の代替（民間・オルタナティブデータ
の活用）の3 段階で整理できる。生成AI は各段階を横断的に後押しする。さらに、企
業を、統計を「出す側」から「使う側」へと位置づけ直し、速報化・詳細化や統計デ
ータの安全な利活用などの便益を返す設計が鍵となる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

はじめに ― 制度の持続可能性と企業の報告者負担 
公的統計は、経済社会の実態を把握する上で重要な公共財である。景気判断、予算編成、社
会保障や地域政策の設計、企業の事業計画に至るまで、その基盤には公的統計がある。しかし
近年、回答の負担の重さなどを背景に、統計調査全体の回収率の低下が世界的な問題となって
おり、日本も例外ではない。 
欧米では、個人・世帯を対象とした世帯系調査だけでなく、企業・事業所を対象とした企業
系調査でも回収率が低下している 1。日本でも、厚生労働省「国民生活基礎調査」 （大規模調査）
の世帯票回収率は1986 年の95.7％から2025 年には62.6％へ低下した。財務省「年次別法人企
業統計調査」2も 2001 年度の 81.8％から 2025 年度の 76.2％へ低下し、特に資本金1,000 万円
未満の小規模法人では72.2％から60.5％への低下が目立つ（図表 1）。

図表 1 統計調査の回収率は低下傾向（ 左：世帯系調査、右：企業系調査 ）

（注）世帯系調査の回収率は「国民生活基礎調査」 （大規模調査） 、企業系調査の回収率は「年次別法人企業統
計調査」 。 
（出所）厚生労働省・財務省より大和総研作成

回収率の低下は、標本の偏りや推計精度の悪化を通じて統計の信頼性に直結するため 、対策
は喫緊の課題である。その背景には後述するように、企業にとって公的統計調査の報告者負担
が重いことがある。この負担は企業の生産性を押し下げる要因となっており看過できない。 
統計を作成する側でも、調査員の減少・高齢化や予算・人員の制約が強まる 3一方、デジタ
ル時代には速報性・詳細性・網羅性への需要が拡大している（図表 2） 。この需給ギャップを埋
める鍵がデジタル化である。

低下やニーズ拡大を受けて大きな曲がり角に」大和総研レポート（2025 年3 月27 日）を参照されたい。 
2 趨勢的な回収率の動向を見るため、長期時系列データを入手できる金融業・保険業を除く系列を用いた。

政府は2007 年に全面改正された統計法（平成19 年法律第53 号）に基づき、おおむね5 年ご
とに公的統計基本計画（以下、基本計画）を策定してきた。現行の第 IV 期基本計画は 2027 年
度に計画期間を終えるため、次期の第 V 期基本計画に向けた検討が今後進むと見込まれる。デ
ジタル時代の公的統計の在り方があらためて問われる。 
今回のレポートでは、とりわけ多くの公的統計への報告に直面する企業の負担の重さという
観点から、前編として統計調査の実態及びデジタル化による「効果」を整理する。そして後編
となる次回のレポートでは、統計調査のデジタル化実現に向けた「課題」と次期の第 V 期基本
計画への提言を行う。

企業が直面する報告者負担の実態 
企業、とりわけ一定規模以上の企業は、複数の基幹統計調査 ・一般統計調査 4の対象となり
得る。例えば、 「経済センサス」 「経済構造実態調査」 「法人企業統計調査」 「毎月勤労統計調査」
「商業動態統計調査」 「サービス産業動態統計調査」 「経済産業省企業活動基本調査」 「科学技術
研究調査」などである（図表3）。 
問題は調査の「数」だけではない。売上高・従業者数・設備投資などの類似項目でも、定義、
対象範囲、対象期間、締め切りが異なり、企業は調査ごとに確認・加工する必要がある。紙・
電子・各府省のシステムが併存し、部署をまたぐ名寄せや転記も生じる5。 
負担が構造的に生じる背景には、日本が各府省でそれぞれ所管分野の統計を作成する「分散
型統計機構」を採用していることがある。調査が府省・分野ごとに個別に設計されるため、府

4 国の行政機関が作成する統計のうち総務大臣が指定する特に重要な統計を「基幹統計」、それ以外を「一般
統計」と呼び、基幹統計を統計調査により作成する「基幹統計調査」とそれ以外の「一般統計調査」の 2 つが
ある。前者の基幹統計調査には報告義務があり、統計法では基幹統計調査の報告（回答）を求められた者が、
報告を拒んだり虚偽の報告をしたりすることを禁止しており（第 13 条）、これらに違反した者に対して、50
万円以下の罰金が定められている（第 61 条）。しかし、罰則の適用は実態として形骸化しているとの報告が
以前よりあり（総務省［2008］「非協力者への処罰（罰則の適用について）」統計委員会基本計画部会第 4 ワ
ーキンググループ会合（第 8 回）配布資料 資料 3-3、平成20 年5 月13 日）、現在の関心は統計行政への協力
を得るための対策に移っている。 
5 総務省［2017］「統計調査の負担感・重複感の実態に関する調査（実施結果）」総務省政策統括官（統計基
準担当）、平成 29 年 5 月 12 日。

速報性 ― より速く 統計調査員の減少・高齢化
詳細性 ― より細かく（地域・業種別 等） 回収率の低下による精度の悪化

省横断の調整が不十分な場合、類似項目の重複や定義の不統一が生じやすい。また、統計法に
より報告義務が課される調査がある一方、回答負担を府省横断で継続的に軽減する仕組みはな
お発展途上にある。

図表 3 企業が回答を求められる主な公的統計調査

経団連の 2021 年の会員企業アンケート 6では、7 割以上が（税・社会保険・登記などの）行
政記録情報の活用は統計調査の回答負担軽減につながると回答した。重複報告の解消は、回答
の遅延や記入誤りを抑え、回収率や統計精度の向上にも寄与する可能性がある。 
しかも、これらの調査は年間を通じて絶えず到来する。毎月の月次調査、毎四半期の四半期
調査、年に一度の年次調査、そして数年ごとの周期調査が重なり合い、企業の統計担当は「常
にどれかの締め切りに追われている」状態に置かれやすい（図表 4） 。負担は特定の時期に集中
するのではなく、通年で恒常的に発生する点にも留意が必要だ。

報告者負担の「構造」を分解する 
負担軽減を論じる前に、企業の報告者負担が何から成るかを分解しておきたい。負担は調査
の「数」だけで測れるものではなく、少なくとも次の3 つの層から成る（図表5）。

図表 5 企業の報告者負担の 3 層構造

（出所）大和総研作成

第一に、直接費用である。調査票の記入・確認・提出に要する時間と人件費であり、最も見
えやすい負担である。回答項目が多く、専門的であるほど費用は膨らむ。 
第二に、間接的・心理的コストである。複数部署にまたがるデータの名寄せ、社内の稟議・
決裁、多数の締め切りの管理、疑義照会への対応など、記入以外に生じる負担であり、直接の
記入時間だけでは捉えにくい大きな負担となる場合がある 7。オンライン化で記入時間が減っ
ても、この層が残れば体感負担は下がりにくい。 
第三に、逆進性である。専任の統計担当を置きにくい中小企業ほど、負担は固定費的に重く
のしかかる。負担の重さは回答の遅延・記入誤り・未回答を招く。前掲の図表 1 右でも小規模
法人ほど回収率が低いことが示されているが、その背景には報告負担に加え、回答体制や調査
への認知など複数の要因が考えられる。負担軽減は、中小を含む幅広い企業の協力を得るため
にも重要である。 
企業の情報提出は、統計調査に加え、財務報告、ESG（環境・社会・ガバナンス） ・サステナ
ビリティ開示、税申告などにも及ぶ。同種データを別々の様式で提出する重複を踏まえれば、
様式・定義の標準化やデータ連携による削減余地は大きい。統計だけでなく企業の「レポーテ
ィング全体」を見据えることが、デジタル化の効果を最大化する。

負担は「測る」ことが可能 ― まず見える化を 
負担軽減の第一歩は測定である。日本でも統計改革の一環として「官民の統計コスト削減」

7 日本経済団体連合会［2021］や総務省［2017］でも、「追加的な作業が必要となる（他部署へのデータ提供
要請等）」「他部門・支店・事業所等へのデータ提供依頼が必要」という点で負担を感じるという回答が最も
多い。 
層 内容 軽減の着眼点
① 直接費用 記入・確認・提出の時間と人件費 電子化・自動転記による入力削減
② 間接的・心理的コスト 名寄せ・稟議・締切管理・疑義対応 共通認証・様式標準化・once-only
③ 逆進性 中小ほど固定費的に重い 行政記録での代替、簡易な回答手段
（横断）レポーティング全体 統計＋財務(XBRL)＋ESG＋税務の重複 定義・様式の標準化とデータ連携

### Page 6

1

6 / 9

に取り組んだ 8が、その後、同じ枠組みによる政府横断的な継続計測は確認できない。共通の
物差しがなければ、議論は感覚論に流れやすい。 
海外では負担計測が制度化されている（ 図表 6）。標準コストモデル（Standard Cost 
Model：SCM）は、情報提供義務への対応にかかる行政手続コスト を、「人件費単価× 所要時間
×対象数×頻度」等により金額換算する手法で、オランダ、デンマーク、英国などが活用して
きた。米国も文書業務削減法（Paperwork Reduction Act：PRA）の下で、行政管理予算局（OMB）
が報告負担時間を集計・公表している。負担の可視化により、施策の優先順位づけと進捗管理
が可能になる。 
日本でも、次期第 V 期基本計画では計測を制度に組み込み、 「測って減らす」PDCAを回すべ
きである。一企業当たりの回答調査数、回答時間、準備・社内調整を含む費用、重複項目数な
どを、統一した定義で企業規模別に把握する 9。これがデジタル化施策の評価と説明責任の基
盤になる。

図表 6 行政手続・統計報告の負担を計測する国内外の枠組み

（出所）総務省、オランダ政府、デンマーク政府、英国政府、米国行政管理予算局資料より大和総研作成

オンライン回答の到達点と限界 
総務省によると 10、オンライン導入率（オンライン回答手段を導入した調査の割合）は2026
年 3 月末で 94.3％に達した。一方、現行の第 IV 期基本計画が掲げる基幹統計調査のオンライ
ン回答率（実際の回答に占めるオンライン回答の割合）の目標（企業系8 割以上、世帯系 5 割
以上）に対し、2025 年度は企業系62.3％、世帯系28.8％にとどまる。選択肢が用意されても、

8 総務省政策統括官（統計制度担当）［2021］「官民の統計コスト削減に係る最終フォローアップ結果」令和
3 年9 月。さらに、統計を含む行政手続全般の負担を測定したものでは、規制改革推進会議行政手続部会

数は、令和 8 年（2026 年）3 月末現在で回答調査客体数が確定している直近の基幹統計調査及び一般統計調査
の数（周期調査等を含む）。

日本 「官民の統計コスト削減」（総務省） 実施者・報告者・利用者のコストを把握（令和3年に最終フォローアップ）
オランダ・デンマーク・英国 等 標準コストモデル（SCM）／削減目標 負担を金額換算し、定量的な削減目標を設定

利用が十分進んでいるとは限らない。 
その背景には、回答者の高齢化やPC 利用率の低さ、設問の多さ、複数部署での分担、紙の方
が一覧性・共有性に優れること、操作・システム上の制約等がある（図表 7） 。単に画面を用意
するだけでは解消しない。 
つまり、オンライン化＝電子化は「入力チャネルを紙から画面に替える」ものにとどまり、
同じ項目を繰り返し入力させる構造そのものは残る。ここに電子化の限界がある。負担を根本
から軽減するには、次で述べる「削減」 「代替」の段階へ進む必要がある。

図表 7 オンライン回答の現状・目標・伸び悩みの要因

（出所）総務省「令和 6 年度・令和 7 年度統計法施行状況報告」より大和総研作成

デジタル化による「効果」 ― 負担軽減の 3 段階 
負担を根本から軽減するデジタル化の効果は、報告負担を段階的に引き下げる形で整理でき
る。すなわち、①調査の電子化（オンライン回答） 、②調査自体の削減（行政記録・企業デー
タの連携） 、③調査の代替（民間・オルタナティブデータの活用）の3 段階である（図表 8）。
①から③へ進むほど、負担軽減の効果は大きくなるが、実現の難易度も高まる。先述したとお
り①の電子化には限界があり、以下では②と③に重点を置いて述べる。

図表 8 デジタル化による報告者負担軽減の 3 段階

（出所）大和総研作成

① 電子化 ― 入力チャネルの電子化 
電子回答は郵送・記入の手間を減らし、入力チェックで誤りも抑える。 「Gビズ ID」11等の共

11 G ビズID は、すべての事業者を対象とする共通認証システムである。アカウントを作成すると、一つのID
とパスワードで複数の行政サービスにログインでき、補助金申請、社会保険手続、許認可に関する申請など、
業務上の各種電子手続に利用できる。詳しくは、デジタル庁「G ビズ ID」を参照。

### Page 8

1

8 / 9

通認証を用いれば認証の手間も軽減できる。しかし、同じ項目を何度も入力させる構造は残る
ため、負担の根本的削減には次の段階が必要である。

② 削減 ― 「そもそも聞かない」 （once-only） 
税・社会保険・登記などの行政記録情報を統計に転用できれば、企業に「そもそも聞かない」
で済む。英国国家統計局（ONS）は付加価値税申告データを短期経済指標に利用している。ま
た、行政が一度取得した情報の再提出を求めない「once-only 原則」は EU のデジタル行政にお
ける重要な原則とされ、対象手続について域内実装が進められている。 
会計・ERP（統合基幹業務システム）・給与などの企業システムから、 API（アプリケーショ
ン・プログラミング・インターフェース）連携や XBRL12で標準様式のデータを提出できれば転
記負担は減る。オランダや豪州の標準ビジネスレポーティング（Standard Business 
Reporting：SBR）は、項目定義と様式を標準化し、複数行政機関への報告を効率化する。さら
に北欧諸国の一部では行政記録を統合した登録簿（レジスター）を統計基盤とし、報告者負担
の抜本的軽減と作成効率化を両立している13。

③ 代替 ― 民間・オルタナティブデータの活用 
POS・スキャナーデータやキャッシュレス決済データ等 は、統計の一部を代替・補完 し、速
報性と詳細性を高める（図表 9） 。日本でも消費者物価指数の一部に POS データが導入されてい
る。また、商業動態統計調査の家電大型専門店分野では POS データ等を用いた提出方法が導入
されている。企業系調査を民間データで代替すれば報告負担を減らせるが、代表性・継続性を
踏まえ、公的統計と補完的に用いる必要がある。

図表 9 国内外のビッグデータ・行政記録の活用事例

（出所）総務省、経済産業省、英国国家統計局（ONS） 、オランダ統計局（CBS） 、米国BEA より大和総研作成

12 XBRL（Extensible Business Reporting Language：拡張可能な事業報告言語）とは、XML（Extensible 
Markup Language）を基礎として、企業の財務・事業報告情報を作成、流通および利用しやすくするために標
準化されたデータ記述言語のこと。 
13 なお、総務省でも現在、事業所母集団データベースの事業所 ID（共通事業所コード）を用いて、報告者の負
担軽減につながる重複排除や高度な分析の推進に関する議論が行われている（総務省「経済統計の基盤整備・
事業所母集団データベースの機能高度化について」統計法等に関する研究会（第 2 回）資料2、2026 年6 月19
日）。 
国 公表元 統計名 データの種類／対象
日本 総務省 消費者物価指数(CPI) POSデータ（家電 等）、ウェブスクレイピング（航空運賃・宿泊料 等）
日本 経済産業省 商業動態統計・丁2調査 POSデータ等による提出・組替集計（家電大型専門店の商品販売額等）
英国 ONS VAT flash estimates（売上高DI） 行政記録情報（HMRCのVAT申告に基づく売上高データ）
オランダ 統計局 消費者物価指数(CPI) スキャナーデータ、ウェブスクレイピング（スーパー、衣料品 等）
米国 BEA Near Real-Time Spending（実験的推計、2024年終了） クレジット・デビット・ギフトカード取引データ

### Page 9

1

9 / 9

生成AI がもたらす「第 4 の層」による横断的な業務支援 
前掲図表 8 で示した3 段階に加え、生成AI は報告者負担の軽減に新たな層を開きつつある。
作業を人手から機械へ移す点で、電子化・削減・代替の各段階を横断的に後押しする。 
統計の報告側（企業・事業所）では、調査票の下書きの自動生成、社内データからの自動転
記・項目マッピング（調査票の項目と対応させること）、用語・定義の自動解説、疑義照会へ
の一次的な自動応答などが期待される。企業が「何を、どの定義で書けばよいか」を調べる手
間を減らし、間接・心理コストの層に効く。 
統計の作成側では、記入内容のAI 審査、産業・職業の自動符号づけ、自由記述の自動分類に
より、審査・集計の効率と正確性を高められる。導入には、精度検証、説明可能性、機密情報
管理を含む運用基準が必要である。 
ただし、生成AI には誤りや品質のばらつきがあり、最終確認は人が担う必要がある。機密情
報の管理と一体で導入し、3 段階の各施策を補強する道具として位置づけるべきである。

「出す側」から「使う側」へ ― 便益の可視化 
負担軽減を持続させる最後の鍵は、企業に協力の見返り（便益）を返すことにある。負担を
求める一方でメリットがなければ、協力は続かない。デジタル化は、提出されたデータを企業
に「返す」便益の設計を可能にする。企業を、統計を「出す側」から「使う側」へと位置づけ
直す発想である。 
・速報化・詳細化：統計をより早く・細かく提供し、企業が自社の景況判断・投資判断に使
いやすくする。 
・統計データの安全な利活用：オンサイト施設やリモートアクセスによる匿名化されたミク
ロデータの研究利用を拡充するとともに、企業が自社の状況を業界平均や同規模企業の集計値
と比較できるベンチマーク情報を提供する。 
・政策の質の向上：EBPM（証拠に基づく政策立案）を通じて、企業の事業環境そのものを改
善する。 
負担論を便益論へ転換することが、企業の継続的な協力の基盤となる。オンライン化だけで
は足りない。once-only と代替（民間・オルタナティブデータの活用）、生成AI、そして便益の
還元をあわせて初めて、企業の負担は根本から軽減され、統計の持続可能性が高まる。後編で
は、これらを阻む課題と、現行の第IV 期基本計画が終了する 2027 年度を見据えた次期第 V 期
基本計画への提言を示す。 
以上

## Document 516: ブラジル大統領選は決選投票を前に一段と接戦の様相をみせる ～初の直接討論会は非難の応酬に終始、市場は選挙後の政策運営を巡_b95eeace

Source PDF: all report pdf/ブラジル大統領選は決選投票を前に一段と接戦の様相をみせる ～初の直接討論会は非難の応酬に終始、市場は選挙後の政策運営を巡_b95eeace.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 3

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 20 日

1/3 
ブラジル大統領選は決選投票を前に一段と接戦の様相をみせる 
～初の直接討論会は非難の応酬に終始、市場は選挙後の政策運営を巡る不透明感を警戒～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ブラジルでは、10 月25 日に実施される大統領選挙の決選投票を前に、10 月 18 日に初めてテ
レビ討論会が実施された。討論会では、ルラ大統領とフラビオ・ボルソナロ氏の間で汚職疑
惑を巡る非難の応酬が目立ち、具体的な政策論争は限定的となった。直近の世論調査におい
ては、両候補の支持率が接近しており、今後はテレビ討論会の影響に加え、支持層の投票参
加の動向が勝敗を左右するとみられる。経済政策の面では、社会保障や公共投資を重視する
ルラ氏と、歳出削減や民間主導の成長を志向するフラビオ氏の違いが鮮明となる一方、財政
健全化と景気下支えの両立は共通の課題となっている。金融市場においては、両候補の支持
率が接近するなど選挙情勢を巡る不透明感が意識されており、選挙後の財政運営や議会との
関係、対外政策の方向性がブラジル経済や金融市場を左右する重要な要因となろう。

【初の直接討論会では汚職疑惑を巡る応酬が目立ち、具体的な政策論争は限定的】 
ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日に実施予定の大統領選挙の決選投票を前に、選挙戦が最終局面を迎え
ている。10 月４日に実施された第１回投票では、ボルソナロ前大統領の長男で右派ＰＬ（自由党）
から出馬したフラビオ・ボルソナロ上院議員が得票率 47.03％、左派ＰＴ（労働者党）から出馬し
た現職のルラ大統領が同 45.16％となり、いずれも過半数に届かなかった。このため、両候補によ
る決選投票が実施されることとなった（注１）。 こうしたなか、10 月 18 日には両者による初めての
テレビ討論会が開催された。 
討論会では、経済政策や汚職問題などが議題となったものの、双方による非難の応酬が目立ち、
具体的な政策を巡る議論は限定的なものとなった。 ルラ大統領は、過去３期にわたる政権運営を通
じた社会保障政策や低所得者層への支援策などの実績を強調した。一方で、フラビオ氏は現政権の
経済運営や汚職問題を批判し、政権交代の必要性を訴えた。特に、両候補に関連する汚職疑惑を巡
る応酬は激しく、ルラ氏が双方の資産・金融取引に関する情報開示を求める場面もみられた。こう
した展開は、ブラジル社会における政治的な分断の根深さを改めて浮き彫りにしたといえる。

注１ 10 月５日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選は決選投票へ、フラビオ氏が首位」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666284.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
【直近の世論調査では両候補の支持率が接近、テレビ討論会の影響を見極める必要がある】 
一方、決選投票に向けた世論調査では、両候補の支持率が接近する動きがみられる。10 月 19 日
に公表された調査では、有効票ベースの支持率はフラビオ氏が 51.1％、ルラ大統領が 48.9％とな
った。10 月９日に公表された前回調査では、それぞれ 52.8％、47.2％であったため、両候補の差
は 5.6ptから 2.2ptに縮小した。さらに、同日に公表された別の調査では、フラビオ氏が 50.3％、
ルラ大統領が49.7％と、統計上の誤差を考慮すれば両候補が拮抗する結果となっている。 
ただし、 これらの調査はテレビ討論会の前に実施されたものであり、 討論会の内容が有権者の投
票行動に与えた影響を直接示すものではないことに注意する必要がある。今後公表される調査にお
いて、支持率の変化が確認されるかが注目される。第１回投票では、棄権率が 21.02％に達してお
り、 政治不信の広がりが有権者の投票参加に及ぼす影響も無視できない。なお、 世論調査によれば、
９割以上の有権者が投票先をすでに決めており、 投票先を変更するつもりはないと回答していると
される。このため、決選投票に向けては、明確な支持を示していない有権者の動向に加え、各候補
が支持層の投票参加をどこまで促すことができるかが重要になる。

【両候補の経済政策を巡る方向性には明確な違い、財政健全化と景気下支えの両立が課題に】 
経済政策を巡っては、両候補の方向性に明確な違いがみられる。ルラ氏は、社会保障政策の拡充
や公共投資を通じた所得再分配と景気下支えを重視しており、 現行の財政運営の枠組みを維持する
姿勢を示している。一方、フラビオ氏は、公的部門の縮小や歳出削減、税負担の軽減を通じた民間
主導の経済成長を志向している。10 月 18 日のテレビ討論会においても、ルラ氏は過去の政権運営
における経済成長や所得再分配の実績を強調したのに対して、フラビオ氏は生活費の上昇や家計債
務の増大、政府債務の拡大を問題視する発言を行っている。 
両候補ともに政府債務の増大に対処する必要性を認識している一方、 財政健全化の実現に向けた
具体的な手法には違いがある。 ルラ氏は経済成長による税収拡大と歳出管理を重視する一方、フラ
ビオ氏は新たな財政ルールの導入や行政経費の削減を打ち出している。とはいえ、 いずれの政策に
ついても、実現に際しては議会との調整が必要となるうえ、 歳出削減や税制改革などが経済活動に
及ぼす影響を見極める必要がある。このため、選挙後の経済政策を評価するうえでは、政策の方向
性だけでなく、財源の裏付けや議会における実現可能性が重要となる。

【金融市場では選挙情勢に加え、財政運営や対外政策を巡る不透明感が意識される展開】 
金融市場においては、 選挙結果によって財政政策や国営企業の経営方針、 対外政策などが変化す
る可能性が意識されている。第１回投票の直後には、フラビオ氏が首位となったことを受けて、通
貨レアル、主要株価指数（ボベスパ指数）ともに大きく上昇するなど、右派政権の誕生を好感する
動きをみせた （注２） 。 しかし、 その後は両候補の支持率が拮抗している様子が確認されていること

注２ 10 月６日付レポート「金融市場はブラジルの右派回帰を好感、株価・レアルともに上昇」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666333.html）

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
を受けて、ともに上値の重い展開となっ
ている （図１） 。また、 長期金利には上昇
圧力がかかる動きが確認されており、財
政運営やインフレ動向を巡る懸念が引き
続き意識される展開となっている。ただ
し、金融市場の動きには国際金融市場や
原油価格の変動なども影響しており、選
挙情勢のみを要因と捉えることは適切で
はない。 
さらに、対外政策を巡っては、両候補
の間で姿勢の違いが明確であり、その行
方が注目される。ルラ政権は、中国をはじめとする新興国との関係やBRICSの枠組みを重視してき
た。一方で、フラビオ氏は米トランプ政権との関係強化を志向する姿勢を強めている。このため、
選挙後の政権運営は、米中対立が続く国際環境において、ブラジルの貿易・投資関係にも影響を及
ぼす可能性が考えられる。とはいえ、中国はブラジルにとって重要な貿易相手国であること、ボル
ソナロ前政権下でも中国との貿易が拡大してきたことを勘案すれば、外交姿勢の変化が直ちに経済
関係の大幅な見直しにつながるとは限らないであろう。 
今後は、 決選投票までに公表される世論調査の動向に加え、 両候補が財政健全化に向けてどの程
度具体的な政策を示すかが注目される。また、10 月 25 日の投票結果が確定した後も、新政権の政
策運営や議会との関係を巡る不透明感が残る可能性がある。 ブラジル経済は、 財政の持続可能性を
確保しつつ、 物価の安定と経済成長を両立させるという課題に直面しており、 選挙後の政策運営が
金融市場の動向を左右する重要な要因となることが予想される。

## Document 533: 中国の貿易統計（26年9月）～輸出入とも前月に続き加速、半導体関連は高い伸びを維持

Source PDF: all report pdf/中国の貿易統計（26年9月）～輸出入とも前月に続き加速、半導体関連は高い伸びを維持.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

１．概況 
・中国海関総署が 2026 年 10 月 14 日に 26 年 9 月の貿易統計の速報値を発表した。財輸出（ドル建
て、図表 1）は前年同月比＋36.3％となった （前月は同＋25％）。財輸入は同＋31％となった （前月
は同＋28.2％）。貿易収支は＋1,335 億ドルと、前年同月（＋904 億ドル）から増加した。当社試算
の季節調整値でみると、輸出は前月比＋10.2％、輸入は同＋7.2％であった。 
・財輸出（人民元建て、図表 2）は前年同月比＋29.6％となった（前月は同＋18.6％）。財輸入は同＋
24.6％となった（前月は同＋21.7％） 。貿易収支は＋9,060 億元と、前年同月（＋6,455 億元）から
増加した。

２．詳細 
（１）輸出 
・国・地域別（ドル建て、前年同月比）では、米国向けで増加が続いており、前月に続き加速した。
ASEAN 向け、日本向け、EU 向けは、いずれも加速した（図表 3）。 
・品目別（同上）では、鋼材や携帯電話が減速した。プラスチック製品や紡績糸等、衣類等、コンピ
ュータ・同部品、家電、自動車等は加速した。集積回路も加速し、3 桁の伸びが続いている（図表
4）。
